166	// Compile-job poll cadence (mirrors useIngestJob): a long ffmpeg stitch must not block the request.
167	const COMPILE_POLL_MIN_MS = 1000;
168	const COMPILE_POLL_MAX_MS = 5000;
169	const COMPILE_POLL_BACKOFF = 1.5;
170	const COMPILE_POLL_CEILING_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000; // a wedged 'running' compile won't poll forever
171	
172	/** Stitch selected rallies into one reel. ASYNC (#329): POST /api/compile returns 202 + a job_id;
173	 *  the ffmpeg stitch runs on the worker and we POLL GET /api/jobs/{id} for the reel — a long stitch
174	 *  would otherwise exceed the ~100 s edge/proxy timeout (524) while the reel actually built. Resolves
175	 *  with the job's CompileResult on success; throws ApiError on a failed/timed-out compile.
176	 *  `locale` (the UI language) localizes the reel watermark engine-side (omitted when absent). */
177	export async function compileReel(
178	  videoId: string,
179	  segmentIds: number[],
180	  outputFilename: string,
181	  cid?: string,
182	  locale?: string,
183	): Promise<CompileResult> {
184	  const c = cid ?? newCorrelationId();
185	  const submitted = await request<ProcessResponse>("/compile", {
186	    method: "POST",
187	    body: JSON.stringify({ video_id: videoId, segment_ids: segmentIds, output_filename: outputFilename, locale }),
188	    cid: c,
189	  });
190	  return pollCompileJob(submitted.job_id, c);
191	}
192	
193	/** Poll a submitted compile job to its terminal state. Returns the reel result on success; throws an
194	 *  ApiError for an engine job-failure, a success-shaped FAILURE (done but the stitch failed / no URL),
195	 *  or a poll timeout — so the caller's existing classifyError/errorText mapping surfaces it like ingest. */
196	async function pollCompileJob(jobId: string, cid: string): Promise<CompileResult> {
197	  const startedAt = Date.now();
198	  let delay = COMPILE_POLL_MIN_MS;
199	  for (;;) {
200	    const job = await getJob(jobId, cid);
201	    if (job.status === "done") {
202	      const result = (job.result ?? {}) as CompileResult;
203	      if (result.status === "failed" || !result.download_url) {
204	        // A real stitch failure carries an engine message — surface it as an `engine` detail (status +
205	        // body set) so classifyError shows it verbatim, NOT the no-status "engine unreachable" fallback.
206	        const msg = result.message || "Highlight stitching failed.";
207	        throw new ApiError(msg, { cid, status: 502, body: msg });
208	      }
209	      return result;
210	    }
211	    if (job.status === "failed") {
212	      const msg = job.error || "Compile job failed.";
213	      throw new ApiError(msg, { cid, status: 502, body: msg });
214	    }
215	    if (Date.now() - startedAt > COMPILE_POLL_CEILING_MS) {
216	      throw new ApiError("Compile timed out.", { cid, status: 504, body: "Compile timed out." });
217	    }
218	    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, delay));
219	    delay = Math.min(delay * COMPILE_POLL_BACKOFF, COMPILE_POLL_MAX_MS);
220	  }
221	}
222	
223	/** The stream/download URL for a compiled reel. GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename} [engine highlights route]. */
224	export function highlightUrl(videoId: string, filename: string): string {
225	  return `${BASE}/highlights/${encodeURIComponent(videoId)}/${encodeURIComponent(filename)}`;
226	}
227	
228	/** Read engine config (for the stitching floor). GET /api/config [engine config route]. */
229	export async function getConfig(cid?: string): Promise<EngineConfig> {
230	  return request<EngineConfig>("/config", { method: "GET", cid });
231	}
232	
233	/** What THIS box can do — drives the first-run wizard. GET /api/capabilities (PACKAGING_PLAN P2c). */
234	export async function getCapabilities(cid?: string): Promise<EngineCapabilities> {
235	  return request<EngineCapabilities>("/capabilities", { method: "GET", cid });
236	}
237	
238	/** The shot-count floor /api/compile silently enforces — the UI must warn, not hide [engine compile floor]. */
239	export function minShotCount(cfg: EngineConfig): number | undefined {
240	  return cfg.stitching?.min_shot_count;
241	}
242	
243	// ── chunked upload (backend/api/uploads.py) ──────────────────────────────────
244	// A browser uploads a LOCAL file in sequential parts ≤ max_part_mb (free-tier edge proxies cap
245	// bodies ~100 MB), then `complete` returns a server-host `path` to feed straight into /api/process.
246	
247	/** Start a chunked upload. 400 = bad extension; 413 = over the size limit [backend/api/uploads.py]. */
248	export async function uploadInit(filename: string, totalSizeBytes: number, cid?: string): Promise<UploadInitResponse> {
249	  return request<UploadInitResponse>("/upload/init", {
250	    method: "POST",
251	    body: JSON.stringify({ filename, total_size_bytes: totalSizeBytes }),
252	    cid,
253	  });
254	}
255	
256	/** Append ONE part (raw bytes — not JSON). Parts are strictly sequential 0,1,2,… [backend/api/uploads.py]. */
257	export async function uploadPart(
258	  uploadId: string,
259	  index: number,
260	  chunk: Blob,
261	  cid?: string,
262	  signal?: AbortSignal,
263	): Promise<UploadPartResponse> {
264	  const c = cid ?? newCorrelationId();
265	  const url = `${BASE}/upload/${encodeURIComponent(uploadId)}/part/${index}`;
266	  log("info", "api.request", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url });
267	  let res: Response;
268	  try {
269	    res = await fetch(url, {
270	      method: "PUT",
271	      body: chunk,
272	      headers: { "content-type": "application/octet-stream", "x-correlation-id": c },
273	      signal,
274	    });
275	  } catch (err) {
276	    log("error", "api.network_error", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url, error: String(err) });
277	    throw new ApiError(`Network error calling PUT ${url}`, { cid: c, cause: err });
278	  }
279	  if (!res.ok) {
280	    const body = await res.text().catch(() => "");
281	    log("error", "api.http_error", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url, status: res.status, body: body.slice(0, 500) });
282	    throw new ApiError(`PUT ${url} failed: ${res.status}`, { cid: c, status: res.status, body });
283	  }
284	  log("info", "api.response", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url, status: res.status });
285	  return (await res.json()) as UploadPartResponse;
286	}
287	
288	/** Finalize: returns {path, video_id} — feed `path` to processVideo() [backend/api/uploads.py]. */
289	export async function uploadComplete(uploadId: string, totalParts: number, cid?: string): Promise<UploadCompleteResponse> {
290	  return request<UploadCompleteResponse>(`/upload/${encodeURIComponent(uploadId)}/complete`, {
291	    method: "POST",
292	    body: JSON.stringify({ total_parts: totalParts }),
293	    cid,
294	  });
295	}
296	
297	/** Abort an in-flight upload (best-effort cleanup of the server-side partial) [backend/api/uploads.py]. */
298	export async function uploadAbort(uploadId: string, cid?: string): Promise<void> {
299	  await request<unknown>(`/upload/${encodeURIComponent(uploadId)}`, { method: "DELETE", cid });
300	}
301	
302	export interface UploadProgress {
303	  sentBytes: number;
304	  totalBytes: number;
305	}
306	
307	/** Upload a local File end to end: init → sequential parts (byte progress, abortable) → complete.
308	 *  On ANY failure or abort it best-effort DELETEs the server-side partial, then rethrows so the
309	 *  caller can classify it (the engine's 400/413 detail survives). Returns {path, video_id}.
310	 *  Note: a per-part 413 (over the per-part limit) aborts the upload server-side — recovery is a
311	 *  fresh init (re-pick the file), which the UI's retry does. */
312	export async function uploadFile(
313	  file: File,
314	  opts: { cid?: string; signal?: AbortSignal; onProgress?: (p: UploadProgress) => void } = {},
315	): Promise<UploadCompleteResponse> {
316	  const cid = opts.cid ?? newCorrelationId();
317	  const init = await uploadInit(file.name, file.size, cid);
318	  // Honor the engine's max_part_mb, but never let a missing/NaN value collapse the math to NaN (which
319	  // would silently upload zero parts) — fall back to the engine's 95 MB default.
320	  const partMb = Number.isFinite(init.max_part_mb) && init.max_part_mb > 0 ? init.max_part_mb : 95;
321	  const partBytes = partMb * 1024 * 1024;
322	  const totalParts = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(file.size / partBytes));
323	  log("info", "upload.start", { cid, filename: file.name, size: file.size, parts: totalParts, part_mb: init.max_part_mb });
324	  let sent = 0;
325	  try {
326	    for (let i = 0; i < totalParts; i++) {
327	      if (opts.signal?.aborted) throw new DOMException("Upload aborted", "AbortError");
328	      const chunk = file.slice(i * partBytes, Math.min((i + 1) * partBytes, file.size));
329	      await uploadPart(init.upload_id, i, chunk, cid, opts.signal);
330	      sent += chunk.size;
331	      opts.onProgress?.({ sentBytes: sent, totalBytes: file.size });
332	    }
333	    const done = await uploadComplete(init.upload_id, totalParts, cid);
334	    log("info", "upload.done", { cid, video_id: done.video_id, path: done.path, size: done.size_bytes });
335	    return done;
336	  } catch (err) {
337	    log("warn", "upload.aborting_partial", { cid, upload_id: init.upload_id, error: String(err) });
338	    await uploadAbort(init.upload_id, cid).catch(() => {});
339	    throw err;
340	  }
341	}

1	// Small pure formatters (node-testable, no React).
2	
3	/** Seconds → "m:ss" (e.g. 65 → "1:05"). Rounds to the nearest second; clamps negatives to 0. */
4	export function mmss(totalSeconds: number): string {
5	  const s = Math.max(0, Math.round(totalSeconds || 0));
6	  const m = Math.floor(s / 60);
7	  return `${m}:${String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0")}`;
8	}
9	
10	/** "3 rallies" / "1 rally" — count with a correctly pluralized noun. */
11	export function pluralize(n: number, one: string, many = one + "s"): string {
12	  return `${n} ${n === 1 ? one : many}`;
13	}
14	
15	/** Turn a user reel name into a safe bare `.mp4` filename for POST /api/compile. The engine's
16	 * `safe_output_filename` (backend/utils/paths.py) rejects path separators / traversal / absolute
17	 * names, so we slugify to [a-z0-9-] and always end in `.mp4`. Empty → "highlights.mp4". */
18	export function reelFilename(name: string): string {
19	  const slug = (name || "")
20	    .toLowerCase()
21	    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
22	    .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, "")
23	    .slice(0, 80);
24	  return `${slug || "highlights"}.mp4`;
25	}

1	// Content regression guards for the localization catalogs — the rules the adversarial translation
2	// audit (#77) established, pinned so the specific bugs can never silently return. This complements
3	// hygiene.test.ts (which proves key/placeholder PARITY) by asserting catalog CONTENT. Each block maps
4	// to a concrete #77 bug class; the mutation that reintroduces the bug fails here. Pure data assertions
5	// over repo-root /locales (D-CATALOG-HOME) — no render context needed.
6	import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
7	import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
8	import { join } from "node:path";
9	
10	const LOCALES_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "..", "..", "locales");
11	
12	type Flat = Record<string, string>;
13	function flatEntries(obj: Record<string, unknown>, prefix = ""): Flat {
14	  const out: Flat = {};
15	  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) {
16	    const key = prefix ? `${prefix}.${k}` : k;
17	    if (v && typeof v === "object" && !Array.isArray(v)) Object.assign(out, flatEntries(v as Record<string, unknown>, key));
18	    else out[key] = String(v);
19	  }
20	  return out;
21	}
22	const load = (loc: string): Flat =>
23	  flatEntries(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, loc, "common.json"), "utf8")) as Record<string, unknown>);
24	
25	const ALL_LOCALES = readdirSync(LOCALES_DIR, { withFileTypes: true })
26	  .filter((d) => d.isDirectory())
27	  .map((d) => d.name);
28	const en = load("en");
29	
30	// (1) Internal-token / debug-tag leaks — EVERY locale. The engine's internal `min_shot_count` config
31	// name leaked into footer.floorSomeRest (#77, kn even wrapped it in <code>). No developer/debug tag
32	// (kbd/samp/tt/var) or that token may reach any user-facing string. <code> is likewise banned —
33	// EXCEPT on the marketing site's developer "own your model" page (site.ownModel.*), which intentionally
34	// renders inline <code> (the rally_annotator.lua filename, the ending_reason enum, <video>.rallies.csv)
35	// through the site shim's data-i18n-html form. That namespace is the sole, deliberate exception; the

37	describe("catalog content — no internal token / debug-tag leaks (every locale)", () => {
38	  const TOKEN = /min_shot_count/;

42	  for (const loc of ALL_LOCALES) {

48	    });
49	  }

63	  for (const loc of ["kn", "hi"] as const) {

69	  }

78	    });
79	  }

83	// jargon term — the Latin word OR an Indic transliteration — is allowed ONLY on a key whose en value
84	// also uses the English word. en avoids these in body copy (uses the brand / "cloud-storage" / passive
85	// voice) EXCEPT the technical ingest.uriAriaLabel + uriPlaceholder; #77 leaked them into kn/hi elsewhere.
86	describe("catalog content — engine/server/bucket jargon only where en uses it (kn/hi)", () => {

1	// CI guardrails that keep i18n "by design" (docs/archives/FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md §4):
2	//   1. KEY-PARITY — every locale catalog has EXACTLY the `en` key set (the extensibility proof:
3	//      drop a stub locale and this lists every gap). Trivially green while only `en` exists; it
4	//      starts enforcing the moment a kn/hi catalog lands (B5/B6).
5	//   2. NO-BARE-STRINGS (attribute level, v1) — user-facing component attributes (placeholder /
6	//      aria-label / title) must route through t(), never a hardcoded literal. A curated start per
7	//      the doc; an AST-level sweep of JSX text can extend it later.
8	import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
9	import { readdirSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs";
10	import { join } from "node:path";
11	
12	// Catalogs are hoisted to repo-root /locales (D-CATALOG-HOME): web/src/i18n -> ../../../locales.
13	const LOCALES_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "..", "..", "locales");
14	const SRC_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "..");
15	
16	function flatten(obj: Record<string, unknown>, prefix = ""): string[] {
17	  const keys: string[] = [];
18	  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) {
19	    const key = prefix ? `${prefix}.${k}` : k;
20	    if (v && typeof v === "object" && !Array.isArray(v)) keys.push(...flatten(v as Record<string, unknown>, key));
21	    else keys.push(key);
22	  }
23	  return keys.sort();
24	}
25	
26	const loadKeys = (locale: string): string[] =>
27	  flatten(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, locale, "common.json"), "utf8")) as Record<string, unknown>);
28	
29	describe("i18n key-parity — every locale has EXACTLY the en keys", () => {
30	  const enKeys = loadKeys("en");
31	  const others = readdirSync(LOCALES_DIR, { withFileTypes: true })
32	    .filter((d) => d.isDirectory() && d.name !== "en")
33	    .map((d) => d.name);
34	
35	  it("en is the non-empty source catalog", () => {
36	    expect(enKeys.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
37	  });
38	
39	  for (const loc of others) {
40	    it(`${loc} matches en exactly (no missing, no extra keys)`, () => {
41	      const keys = loadKeys(loc);
42	      const missing = enKeys.filter((k) => !keys.includes(k));
43	      const extra = keys.filter((k) => !enKeys.includes(k));
44	      expect({ locale: loc, missing, extra }).toEqual({ locale: loc, missing: [], extra: [] });
45	    });
46	  }
47	});
48	
49	// Key-parity proves every locale has the same KEYS; this proves every locale's VALUE keeps the same
50	// simple `{placeholder}` set as en — so a translation can never silently drop a {count}/{error}/{min}/
51	// {filename}/{n}/… and break interpolation. (ICU plural headers like `{count, plural, …}` carry no
52	// simple {word} placeholder and match as empty on both sides, so they're correctly ignored.)
53	describe("placeholder parity — every locale preserves en's {placeholders}", () => {
54	  function flatEntries(obj: Record<string, unknown>, prefix = ""): Record<string, string> {
55	    const out: Record<string, string> = {};
56	    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) {
57	      const key = prefix ? `${prefix}.${k}` : k;
58	      if (v && typeof v === "object" && !Array.isArray(v)) Object.assign(out, flatEntries(v as Record<string, unknown>, key));
59	      else out[key] = String(v);
60	    }
61	    return out;
62	  }
63	  const placeholders = (s: string) => (s.match(/\{(\w+)\}/g) ?? []).sort();
64	  const load = (loc: string) => flatEntries(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, loc, "common.json"), "utf8")));
65	  const enE = load("en");
66	  const others = readdirSync(LOCALES_DIR, { withFileTypes: true })
67	    .filter((d) => d.isDirectory() && d.name !== "en")
68	    .map((d) => d.name);
69	
70	  for (const loc of others) {
71	    it(`${loc} preserves every {placeholder} that en uses`, () => {
72	      const locE = load(loc);
73	      const mismatches: Array<{ key: string; en: string[]; got: string[] }> = [];
74	      for (const key of Object.keys(enE)) {
75	        if (!(key in locE)) continue; // missing keys are the key-parity test's job
76	        const a = placeholders(enE[key]);
77	        const b = placeholders(locE[key]);
78	        if (a.join(",") !== b.join(",")) mismatches.push({ key, en: a, got: b });
79	      }
80	      expect(mismatches).toEqual([]);
81	    });
82	  }
83	});
84	
85	describe("es 3-form plural (CLDR one/many/other) — MULTILINGUAL Phase A", () => {
86	  // Spanish cardinal is {one, many, other}; every es ICU plural message MUST carry a `many {` arm
87	  // (the `many` category fires on large/compact numbers) or intl-messageformat silently falls back.
88	  // These are the plural-bearing keys in the catalog.
89	  const es = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, "es", "common.json"), "utf8")) as Record<string, Record<string, string>>;
90	  const pluralKeys: Array<[string, string]> = [
91	    ["footer", "count"],
92	    ["footer", "floorSomeLead"],
93	    ["selection", "count"],
94	  ];
95	  for (const [ns, key] of pluralKeys) {
96	    it(`es ${ns}.${key} carries one/many/other arms`, () => {
97	      const msg = es[ns][key];
98	      expect(msg, `${ns}.${key}`).toMatch(/\bone\s*\{/);
99	      expect(msg, `${ns}.${key}`).toMatch(/\bmany\s*\{/);
100	      expect(msg, `${ns}.${key}`).toMatch(/\bother\s*\{/);
101	    });
102	  }
103	});
104	
105	describe("no-bare-strings — user-facing component attributes go through t()", () => {
106	  const FILES = [
107	    "App.tsx",
108	    "components/QueryBar.tsx",
109	    "components/TapFilters.tsx",
110	    "components/RallyGrid.tsx",
111	    "components/SelectionFooter.tsx",
112	    "components/CompileResult.tsx",
113	    "components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx",
114	    "components/IngestPanel.tsx",
115	    "components/SetupWizard.tsx",
116	    "components/VideoLibrary.tsx",
117	    "components/AnnotatePanel.tsx",
118	  ];
119	  // A hardcoded placeholder / aria-label / title with letters is a bare-string regression — it
120	  // must be an expression ({t(...)}), not a quoted literal.
121	  const BARE_ATTR = /\b(?:placeholder|aria-label|title)\s*=\s*"[^"]*[A-Za-z]{2}[^"]*"/g;
122	
123	  for (const f of FILES) {
124	    it(`${f}: no hardcoded placeholder/aria-label/title`, () => {
125	      const src = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, f), "utf8");
126	      expect(src.match(BARE_ATTR) ?? []).toEqual([]);
127	    });
128	  }
129	});
130	
131	describe("Indic fonts (B4) — self-hosted OFL Noto, no runtime CDN", () => {
132	  it("main.tsx bundles the OFL Noto Kannada + Devanagari fonts (self-hosted, not Google Fonts)", () => {
133	    const main = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, "main.tsx"), "utf8");
134	    expect(main).toMatch(/@fontsource-variable\/noto-sans-kannada/);
135	    expect(main).toMatch(/@fontsource-variable\/noto-sans-devanagari/);
136	  });
137	  it("the font stack falls back to Noto for Kannada + Devanagari glyphs", () => {
138	    const css = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, "index.css"), "utf8");

140	    expect(css).toMatch(/Noto Sans Devanagari Variable/);
141	  });
142	});
143	
144	// The Studio SPA must render correctly on an AIR-GAPPED appliance (GPU/CPU/RAM, no internet) —

146	describe("offline / self-contained — no external runtime assets", () => {

152	  ];
153	  for (const rel of FILES) {

156	    });

160	    expect(html).not.toMatch(/(?:href|src)\s*=\s*["']https?:\/\//i);

1	// @vitest-environment jsdom
2	//
3	// The upload state machine (T4): start(file) → uploading(%) → hand the server PATH to onComplete |
4	// error. Pins: an empty file is rejected before any network call, a happy upload calls onComplete
5	// with the returned path + filename and returns to idle, and an engine rejection surfaces a
6	// classified error.
7	import { describe, it, expect, vi, afterEach } from "vitest";
8	import { renderHook, act, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
9	import { useUpload } from "./useUpload";
10	
11	type FetchFn = typeof fetch;
12	const MB = 1024 * 1024;
13	const ok = (j: unknown) => ({ ok: true, status: 200, json: async () => j, text: async () => JSON.stringify(j) });
14	const httpErr = (status: number, detail: string) => ({
15	  ok: false, status, json: async () => ({ detail }), text: async () => JSON.stringify({ detail }),
16	});
17	
18	function uploadFetch(opts: { partFailsAt?: number } = {}): FetchFn {
19	  return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
20	    const u = String(url);
21	    const m = init?.method ?? "GET";
22	    if (u === "/api/upload/init") return ok({ upload_id: "up1", max_part_mb: 1, next_part: "/api/upload/up1/part/0" });
23	    const part = u.match(/\/part\/(\d+)$/);
24	    if (part && m === "PUT") {
25	      if (opts.partFailsAt === Number(part[1])) return httpErr(413, "Part exceeds the per-part limit");
26	      return ok({ upload_id: "up1", received_parts: 1, received_bytes: (init?.body as Blob).size });
27	    }
28	    if (u.endsWith("/complete")) return ok({ path: "/srv/uploads/match.mp4", video_id: "VID", size_bytes: 1 });
29	    if (m === "DELETE") return ok({ status: "aborted" });
30	    throw new Error(`unexpected ${m} ${u}`);
31	  }) as unknown as FetchFn;
32	}
33	
34	const videoFile = (bytes: number, name = "match.mp4") => new File([new Uint8Array(bytes)], name, { type: "video/mp4" });
35	
36	afterEach(() => {
37	  vi.restoreAllMocks();
38	  vi.unstubAllGlobals();
39	});
40	
41	describe("useUpload", () => {
42	  it("rejects an empty file before any network call", () => {
43	    const f = vi.fn();
44	    vi.stubGlobal("fetch", f as unknown as FetchFn);
45	    const onComplete = vi.fn();
46	    const { result } = renderHook(() => useUpload(onComplete));
47	
48	    act(() => result.current.start(videoFile(0)));
49	
50	    expect(result.current.phase).toEqual({ status: "error", error: { code: "invalidUri" } });
51	    expect(f).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
52	    expect(onComplete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
53	  });
54	
55	  it("uploads a file and hands the returned server path and filename to onComplete", async () => {
56	    vi.stubGlobal("fetch", uploadFetch());
57	    const onComplete = vi.fn();
58	    const { result } = renderHook(() => useUpload(onComplete));
59	
60	    act(() => result.current.start(videoFile(0.3 * MB)));
61	    await waitFor(() => expect(onComplete).toHaveBeenCalledWith("/srv/uploads/match.mp4", "match.mp4"));
62	    expect(result.current.phase.status).toBe("idle"); // handed off — the engine phase takes over
63	  });
64	
65	  it("surfaces a classified error when a part is rejected", async () => {
66	    vi.stubGlobal("fetch", uploadFetch({ partFailsAt: 0 }));
67	    const onComplete = vi.fn();
68	    const { result } = renderHook(() => useUpload(onComplete));
69	
70	    act(() => result.current.start(videoFile(0.3 * MB)));
71	    await waitFor(() => expect(result.current.phase.status).toBe("error"));
72	    expect(onComplete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
73	    // a 413 with a {detail} body classifies as an engine error (its message survives)
74	    expect(result.current.phase).toMatchObject({ status: "error", error: { code: "engine" } });
75	  });
76	});

### 29. user (2026-07-04T01:30:58.237Z)

1	// @vitest-environment jsdom
2	//
3	// The ingest screen end to end: type a URI → submit → (mocked engine) → onLoaded gets the video_id;
4	// and the two failure surfaces a non-technical user actually hits — the engine's own 400 detail,
5	// and a friendly "is the engine running?" when it's unreachable. Mounts the real i18n catalog.
6	import { describe, it, expect, vi, beforeEach, afterEach } from "vitest";
7	import { render, screen, fireEvent, cleanup, act, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
8	import i18n from "../i18n/index";
9	import { IngestPanel } from "./IngestPanel";
10	import type { JobStatus } from "../api/types";
11	
12	type FetchFn = typeof fetch;
13	
14	function httpOk(status: number, json: unknown) {
15	  return { ok: status >= 200 && status < 300, status, json: async () => json, text: async () => JSON.stringify(json) };
16	}
17	
18	function fetchSeq(process: unknown, jobs: JobStatus[]): FetchFn {
19	  let i = 0;
20	  return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
21	    const u = String(url);
22	    // The panel probes /api/capabilities on mount (compute-picker) — answer it benignly so the
23	    // ingest-flow assertions don't see a stray "unexpected fetch". No cloud ⇒ no picker.
24	    if (u === "/api/capabilities") return httpOk(200, {});
25	    if (u === "/api/process" && (init?.method ?? "GET") === "POST") return process;
26	    if (u.startsWith("/api/jobs/")) {
27	      const body = jobs[Math.min(i, jobs.length - 1)];
28	      i += 1;
29	      return httpOk(200, body);
30	    }
31	    throw new Error(`unexpected fetch ${u}`);
32	  }) as unknown as FetchFn;
33	}
34	
35	/** Like fetchSeq but lets a test set the capabilities payload + capture the /api/process body — used
36	 *  to drive and assert the compute-picker. */
37	function fetchAll(opts: {
38	  caps?: unknown;
39	  process?: unknown;
40	  jobs?: JobStatus[];
41	  onProcessBody?: (body: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
42	}): FetchFn {
43	  let i = 0;
44	  const jobs = opts.jobs ?? [];
45	  return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
46	    const u = String(url);
47	    if (u === "/api/capabilities") return httpOk(200, opts.caps ?? {});
48	    if (u === "/api/process" && (init?.method ?? "GET") === "POST") {
49	      opts.onProcessBody?.(JSON.parse(String(init?.body ?? "{}")) as Record<string, unknown>);
50	      return opts.process ?? httpOk(202, { job_id: "j", status: "queued", poll: "/api/jobs/j" });
51	    }
52	    if (u.startsWith("/api/jobs/")) {
53	      const body = jobs[Math.min(i, jobs.length - 1)];
54	      i += 1;
55	      return httpOk(200, body);
56	    }
57	    throw new Error(`unexpected fetch ${u}`);
58	  }) as unknown as FetchFn;
59	}
60	
61	beforeEach(async () => {
62	  window.localStorage.clear();
63	  window.history.replaceState(null, "", "/");
64	  await i18n.changeLanguage("en"); // guarantees init completed + a known locale for the assertions
65	});
66	
67	afterEach(() => {
68	  cleanup();

238	        { id: "gdrive", kind: "gdrive", label: "Drive", usable: false, free_gb: null },
239	      ],
240	    },
241	  };
242	
243	  // A fetch mock that answers the full store→process choreography (upload → assets → process → job).
244	  function fetchStore(opts: {
245	    caps?: unknown;
246	    jobs?: JobStatus[];
247	    onAssetBody?: (b: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
248	    onProcessBody?: (b: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
249	  }): FetchFn {
250	    let ji = 0;
251	    const jobs = opts.jobs ?? [];
252	    return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
253	      const u = String(url);
254	      const m = init?.method ?? "GET";
255	      if (u === "/api/capabilities") return httpOk(200, opts.caps ?? {});
256	      if (u === "/api/upload/init") return httpOk(200, { upload_id: "up1", max_part_mb: 95, next_part: "/api/upload/up1/part/0" });
257	      if (/^\/api\/upload\/up1\/part\/\d+$/.test(u)) return httpOk(200, { upload_id: "up1", received_parts: 1, received_bytes: 3 });
258	      if (/^\/api\/upload\/up1\/complete$/.test(u)) return httpOk(200, { path: "/srv/up/match.mp4", video_id: "mock", size_bytes: 3 });
259	      if (u === "/api/assets" && m === "POST") {
260	        opts.onAssetBody?.(JSON.parse(String(init?.body ?? "{}")) as Record<string, unknown>);
261	        return httpOk(201, { video_id: "vidS", medium: "gcs", stored_uri: "gcs://b/videos/vidS.mp4", copied: true, status: "stored" });
262	      }
263	      if (u === "/api/process" && m === "POST") {
264	        opts.onProcessBody?.(JSON.parse(String(init?.body ?? "{}")) as Record<string, unknown>);
265	        return httpOk(202, { job_id: "j", status: "queued", poll: "/api/jobs/j" });
266	      }
267	      if (u.startsWith("/api/jobs/")) {
268	        const b = jobs[Math.min(ji, jobs.length - 1)];
269	        ji += 1;
270	        return httpOk(200, b);
271	      }
272	      throw new Error(`unexpected fetch ${m} ${u}`);
273	    }) as unknown as FetchFn;
274	  }
275

1	import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";
2	import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react";
3	import tailwindcss from "@tailwindcss/vite";
4	
5	// The SPA makes SAME-ORIGIN relative `/api/*` calls. In the appliance (prod) a reverse
6	// proxy serves the SPA and proxies `/api` to the engine on 127.0.0.1:8000
7	// (docs/LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md). In dev, Vite proxies `/api` to the local engine
8	// (`python -m backend.main --server`, :8000). Override with VITE_ENGINE_ORIGIN if needed.
9	export default defineConfig({
10	  plugins: [react(), tailwindcss()],
11	  server: {
12	    proxy: {
13	      "/api": { target: "http://localhost:8000", changeOrigin: true },
14	    },
15	  },
16	  test: {
17	    // Default env is node (the pure lib/parser tests); the i18n render test opts into jsdom via a
18	    // `// @vitest-environment jsdom` docblock. Match .test.tsx too so render tests are discovered.
19	    environment: "node",
20	    include: ["src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}"],
21	    // DOM-env localStorage polyfill (jsdom under Node 26 ships none); node-env files no-op.
22	    setupFiles: ["src/test/setup.ts"],

+export type IngestErrorCode = "invalidUri" | "networkError" | "emptyResult" | "timeout" | "fileTooLarge" | "engine";
 
 export interface IngestError {
   code: IngestErrorCode;
   /** Present for `engine` — the verbatim FastAPI detail / status; rendered as-is, not translated. */
   detail?: string;
+  /** Present for `fileTooLarge` — the picked file's size and the engine's cap, both in BYTES, so the
+   *  localized message can show both ("This clip is 5.8 GB, over the 4 GB upload limit…"). */
+  sizeBytes?: number;
+  capBytes?: number;
 }
 
 /** Classify a thrown client error into a stable code. A network throw (ApiError with no `status`)
diff --git a/web/src/lib/format.ts b/web/src/lib/format.ts
index ff74437..84e9b53 100644
--- a/web/src/lib/format.ts
+++ b/web/src/lib/format.ts
@@ -12,6 +12,19 @@ export function pluralize(n: number, one: string, many = one + "s"): string {
   return `${n} ${n === 1 ? one : many}`;
 }
 
+/** Bytes → a short human size for user copy (the upload size-guard message). ≥ 1 GB renders as GB
+ *  with up to one decimal, trailing ".0" trimmed ("4 GB", "5.8 GB"); smaller renders as whole MB
+ *  ("512 MB"). Clamps negatives/NaN to 0 and never returns below "1 MB" for a positive size. */
+export function formatFileSize(bytes: number): string {
+  const b = Math.max(0, Number.isFinite(bytes) ? bytes : 0);
+  const gb = b / 1024 ** 3;
+  if (gb >= 1) {
+    const rounded = Math.round(gb * 10) / 10;
+    return `${Number.isInteger(rounded) ? rounded : rounded.toFixed(1)} GB`;
+  }
+  return `${Math.max(1, Math.round(b / 1024 ** 2))} MB`;
+}
+
 /** Turn a user reel name into a safe bare `.mp4` filename for POST /api/compile. The engine's
  * `safe_output_filename` (backend/utils/paths.py) rejects path separators / traversal / absolute
  * names, so we slugify to [a-z0-9-] and always end in `.mp4`. Empty → "highlights.mp4". */

### 116. assistant (2026-07-04T01:45:48.118Z)

No existing memory/handoff for this project — nothing to update, and a single self-contained PR is fully recorded in git, so I won't add memory noise.

## Done — PR #153

Added a client-side upload-size guard to the ingest SPA: an oversized file now fails **instantly, before any network round-trip**, with a size-specific, localized message + a "trim/chunk it first" hint — replacing the old "uploading" → generic engine-error flip.

🔗 **https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/153**

### What changed (`web/src/`)
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| [api/types.ts](web/src/api/types.ts) | `max_upload_mb?` added to `EngineCapabilities` |
| [lib/errors.ts](web/src/lib/errors.ts) | new `"fileTooLarge"` `IngestErrorCode` carrying `sizeBytes` + `capBytes` (bytes) |
| [lib/format.ts](web/src/lib/format.ts) | `formatFileSize()` → `"5.8 GB"` / `"512 MB"` |
| [hooks/useUpload.ts](web/src/hooks/useUpload.ts) | size guard right after the 0-byte reject; **no network** when it fires; cap threaded in via a param (held in a ref so `start` stays stable); stays i18n-free (returns the code) |
| [components/IngestPanel.tsx](web/src/components/IngestPanel.tsx) | reads the cap from capabilities, threads it into `useUpload`; existing `errorText` maps the code → localized string |
| 6 locale catalogs | `errors.fileTooLarge` in en/kn/hi/es/da/id, referencing `scripts/chunk_video.py` + the Google Drive path |

Example (en): *"This clip is 5.8 GB, over the 4 GB upload limit. Trim it to a shorter highlight first — see scripts/chunk_video.py (or use the Google Drive path)."*

### Design notes
- I put the code→prose mapping in `errorText.ts` (the shared "one place a classified error becomes user-facing text"), which `IngestPanel` already calls — so the hook stays i18n-free per its contract and the message interpolates size + cap at the view layer.
- Guard is opt-in on the cap: absent/0 `max_upload_mb` ⇒ guard skipped, and the engine's server-side 413 fail-fast still applies.

### Verification
- **typecheck**: clean (`tsc --noEmit`)
- **tests**: **291 passed** (30 files) — includes hook pre-network reject + within-cap upload, `errorText` formatting, `formatFileSize`, and a component-level 5 GB-vs-4 GB reject asserting no upload/process call
- **coverage**: 94.73% stmts / 88.08% branches / 89.65% funcs / 94.73% lines — above the CI floor (92/87/84/92), branches non-regressing; key/placeholder-parity + catalog-invariant i18n guards green
- **lint**: this repo has no ESLint — the SPA's CI lint gate is strict `tsc`, which passes

I verified behavior through the automated suite (the component test renders the real `IngestPanel` with the real i18n catalog) rather than a browser preview — meaningfully exercising it live would need a running engine that reports `max_upload_mb` plus picking a >4 GB file, which the tests cover deterministically instead.

Two things worth flagging: I depended only on `max_upload_mb`, so this PR assumes the companion engine change that exposes it in `/api/capabilities` lands; and I caught early that a stray path had me editing the main-repo checkout instead of the worktree — corrected, and confirmed the main repo is clean with all changes contained to the branch.

### 160. assistant (2026-07-04T02:02:48.168Z)

Done. Both trees are clean, the main-repo checkout is untouched, and PR #153 now has both commits.

## Summary

I extended the fix so a user who **doesn't know how to trim** gets actual, self-service help — not a Python script path.

**The problem with the first version:** the message led with `scripts/chunk_video.py`, which doesn't even exist in this repo (it's in the engine repo), and means nothing to a badminton coach. Two constraints shaped the solution: the SPA must run **air-gapped** (the hygiene tests forbid external links/CDN), so I couldn't link out to a how-to; and the SPA renders formatted strings via `<Trans>`, so I could use inline `<b>` lead-ins.

**What I added** — a keyboard-accessible native `<details>` **"How do I trim it?"** disclosure in the `fileTooLarge` error card ([IngestPanel.tsx](web/src/components/IngestPanel.tsx)):
- **On a phone** — trim in Photos/Gallery (drag the end handles), then upload the shorter clip.
- **On a computer** — trim in Windows Photos or Mac QuickTime Player (both trim natively — I dropped a VLC suggestion because it has no beginner-friendly trim).
- **Advanced** — split with `scripts/chunk_video.py`, or upload the whole video to Google Drive.

I also softened the top-line message so it no longer leads with dev jargon: *"This clip is 5.8 GB, just over the 4 GB upload limit, so trim it to your best rally or two and upload that shorter version."*

**How I built the copy:** I ran a multi-agent workflow — a 3-way judge panel refined the English, then per-locale agents translated into all six languages, each adversarially verified against the catalog's linter rules (placeholder/key parity, kn/hi jargon bans, no `<code>` tags, ICU-apostrophe pitfalls). All five non-English locales passed; the Kannada verifier caught and fixed a real orthography bug (a malformed virama on the independent vowel in "app", `ಆ್ಯಪ್`→`ಆಪ್`) that would have rendered as a broken glyph.

**Verification:** typecheck clean; **291 tests pass**; coverage 94.76/88.11/89.65/94.76 — above the CI floor (92/87/84/92), no regression. The component test renders the real `IngestPanel` with the real i18n catalog and asserts the disclosure shows the phone/computer/advanced guidance while making zero network calls.

One judgment call worth flagging: I moved the `scripts/chunk_video.py` reference out of the headline message and into the "Advanced" help line. It's still on the same error surface (satisfying the original "reference the chunk helper" requirement), but it no longer greets a non-technical coach first — which is the whole point of your follow-up.

I verified through the automated suite rather than a browser preview: meaningfully exercising this live needs a running engine that reports `max_upload_mb` plus picking a >4 GB file, which the tests simulate deterministically instead.

FILE: web/src/hooks/useUpload.ts:52  (avidullu)
DIFF HUNK:
@@ -39,6 +43,14 @@ export function useUpload(onComplete: (path: string, filename: string) => void)
         setPhase({ status: "error", error: { code: "invalidUri" } });
         return;
       }
+      // Oversized file: fail INSTANTLY (no wasted round-trip) — /upload/init would 413 anyway. Carry
+      // the size + cap so IngestPanel can localize a "trim/chunk it first" message. No cap ⇒ skip the
+      // guard (the engine still fail-fasts server-side).
+      const cap = maxUploadBytesRef.current;
+      if (cap && file.size > cap) {
+        setPhase({ status: "error", error: { code: "fileTooLarge", sizeBytes: file.size, capBytes: cap } });
+        return;

COMMENT:
Because this returns before `stop()`, a previous upload stays alive if the user repicks an oversized file while another upload is already in flight. That older async task can still finish and call `onCompleteRef.current(...)`, so we can end up processing file A after showing the `fileTooLarge` error for file B. Could we abort first (or move `stop()` ahead of the early-return guards) and add a regression test for `small upload in flight -> pick oversized file`?

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FILE: web/src/lib/format.ts:22  (avidullu)
DIFF HUNK:
@@ -12,6 +12,19 @@ export function pluralize(n: number, one: string, many = one + "s"): string {
   return `${n} ${n === 1 ? one : many}`;
 }
 
+/** Bytes → a short human size for user copy (the upload size-guard message). ≥ 1 GB renders as GB
+ *  with up to one decimal, trailing ".0" trimmed ("4 GB", "5.8 GB"); smaller renders as whole MB
+ *  ("512 MB"). Clamps negatives/NaN to 0 and never returns below "1 MB" for a positive size. */
+export function formatFileSize(bytes: number): string {
+  const b = Math.max(0, Number.isFinite(bytes) ? bytes : 0);
+  const gb = b / 1024 ** 3;
+  if (gb >= 1) {
+    const rounded = Math.round(gb * 10) / 10;

COMMENT:
This rounding can make the rejected file and the cap display as the same value right at the threshold. With a 4 GiB cap, anything up to roughly 50 MiB over still formats as `4 GB`, so the new error can read like "This clip is 4 GB, just over the 4 GB upload limit" even though the two numbers look identical. We probably need either more precision here or comparison-aware formatting when `sizeBytes > capBytes` but the rounded strings match.

### 187. assistant (2026-07-04T03:08:18.655Z)

Done. Here's the review and what I did about it.

## The review (from avidullu on PR #153)

A top-level "I like the direction" with **two inline findings — both genuine correctness bugs**, now fixed in commit `00109b8` (pushed) with replies on each thread.

### 1. `useUpload.ts` — supersede race
**Finding:** the 0-byte and oversized guards `return` *before* `stop()`. So if a small upload is already in flight and the user then picks an oversized file, the old upload is never aborted — it can finish and call `onComplete(...)`, processing file A *after* the UI has shown a `fileTooLarge` error for file B.

**Fix:** moved `stop()` to the very top of `start()`, before any guard. A superseding pick now aborts the prior upload first; once its controller is aborted, the old async task hits its `signal.aborted` checks and returns without touching `onComplete` or the error phase. This also closes the same latent race on the pre-existing 0-byte guard. Added the requested regression test: *small upload in flight → pick oversized → assert `onComplete` never fires and the phase stays `fileTooLarge`.*

### 2. `format.ts` — threshold display collision
**Finding:** rounding can make the file and the cap render identically — a file up to ~50 MiB over a 4 GiB cap reads *"This clip is 4 GB, just over the 4 GB upload limit."*

**Fix:** added `formatSizeOverCap(size, cap)`. When the normal short forms collide, it re-renders **both** in a finer unit (whole MB, then raw bytes) so they always read differently and the size still reads as the larger. `errorText` uses it for the message. At the threshold, a 4 GiB cap + ~30 MiB file now reads *"4126 MB … 4096 MB"* instead of *"4 GB … 4 GB."* Tests cover the clear case, the GB collision, and the sub-MB bytes fallback.

## Verification
- `tsc` clean; **295 tests pass** (+4 new).
- Coverage **94.79 / 88.52 / 89.79 / 94.79** — up on every axis vs. before (branches 88.11→88.52), above the CI floor (92/87/84/92). `format.ts` and `errorText.ts` are now 100% covered.

Both fixes are on the branch and the reviewer's threads have replies pointing at commit `00109b8`. The reviewer caught two real bugs I'd missed — the race in particular was a genuine correctness hole, not a style nit.

1	// DRIFT GUARD for the one build step the otherwise no-build site has (tracker B3 / D-CATALOG-HOME):
2	// the catalogs the marketing site ships (site/public/locales/) MUST be byte-identical to the single
3	// source of truth (repo-root /locales/). If someone edits /locales without re-running the copy, this
4	// fails CI with the exact fix. Run `npm run copy:locales` to resync.
5	import { test } from "node:test";
6	import assert from "node:assert/strict";
7	import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
8	import { join } from "node:path";
9	
10	const SRC = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "..", "locales");
11	const PUB = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "public", "locales");
12	
13	test("site/public/locales mirrors repo-root /locales exactly (else: npm run copy:locales)", () => {
14	  const locales = readdirSync(SRC, { withFileTypes: true }).filter((d) => d.isDirectory()).map((d) => d.name);
15	  assert.ok(locales.length >= 3, "expected at least en/kn/hi catalogs in /locales");
16	
17	  const shipped = new Set(readdirSync(PUB, { withFileTypes: true }).filter((d) => d.isDirectory()).map((d) => d.name));
18	  for (const loc of locales) {
19	    const src = readFileSync(join(SRC, loc, "common.json"), "utf8");
20	    assert.ok(shipped.has(loc), `site/public/locales/${loc} is missing — run npm run copy:locales`);
21	    const pub = readFileSync(join(PUB, loc, "common.json"), "utf8");
22	    assert.equal(pub, src, `site/public/locales/${loc}/common.json drifted from /locales/${loc} — run npm run copy:locales`);
23	  }
24	  // No stale extra locales shipped that aren't in the source of truth.
25	  for (const loc of shipped) {
26	    assert.ok(locales.includes(loc), `site/public/locales/${loc} has no source in /locales — run npm run copy:locales`);
27	  }
28	});

### 198. user (2026-07-04T03:14:35.897Z)

1	// Mirror the single-source-of-truth catalogs (repo-root /locales, D-CATALOG-HOME) into the site's
2	// static public/ dir, so the dependency-free marketing site can fetch them at runtime via the
3	// t()/data-i18n shim (public/i18n.js). This copy is the ONLY build step the otherwise no-build site
4	// has. Run on demand (`npm run copy:locales`) or automatically before deploy (`predeploy`).
5	//
6	// The committed copy under site/public/locales/ is what actually ships (static hosts serve it with
7	// no build); the drift-guard test (src/locales-drift.test.js) fails CI if it goes stale vs /locales.
8	import { cpSync, rmSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
9	import { join } from "node:path";
10	
11	const SRC = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "..", "locales");
12	const DEST = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "public", "locales");
13	
14	rmSync(DEST, { recursive: true, force: true });
15	mkdirSync(DEST, { recursive: true });
16	cpSync(SRC, DEST, { recursive: true });
17	
18	console.log(`copy-locales: ${readdirSync(DEST).join(", ")} -> site/public/locales/`);
19

## What the latest comments said, and what I did

The two inline code findings were already resolved in my prior commit — the reviewer confirmed at 03:12: *"the supersede-abort change and the threshold-formatting follow-up both look good."* But they flagged a **remaining CI blocker** I had missed:

**The `site` job was red.** The marketing site ships a byte-identical mirror of the locale catalogs under `site/public/locales/*`, guarded by `src/locales-drift.test.js`. I'd edited the source `/locales/*/common.json` (the `fileTooLarge` + trim-help strings) across three commits but never re-ran the copy step — so the mirror had drifted:
> `site/public/locales/da/common.json drifted from /locales/da — run npm run copy:locales`

**Fix (commit `c5abf0d`):** ran `npm run copy:locales` from `site/` and committed the 6 regenerated mirror files. Verified locally — the drift test passes and the full site suite is green (95/95). I replied to the reviewer's comment confirming the fix.

CI has since re-run on the new head and **all four checks pass**. The PR is now green end-to-end, with both original code findings addressed and confirmed by the reviewer.

One honest note: this drift was my miss from the very first commit — the repo has a single-source-of-truth convention for locales with a build step (`copy:locales`) I should have run as soon as I touched `/locales`. Good to have the guardrail; I've now internalized that any `/locales` edit in this repo needs the mirror resync.

### What shipped
An instant client-side oversized-upload guard for the ingest SPA — a file over the engine's cap fails before any network round-trip, with a localized message and a keyboard-accessible **"How do I trim it?"** disclosure (phone/computer steps for a coach, plus the advanced `scripts/chunk_video.py` / Google Drive route), all inline so it works air-gapped. Includes the two review fixes (stop-first supersede race, comparison-aware threshold formatting) and the resynced `site/public/locales` mirror.

A couple of notes for cleanup: the remote branch `claude/zealous-pike-7d0a85` still exists (the repo doesn't auto-delete on merge, and I avoided `--delete-branch` since we're sitting in its worktree) — you can delete it whenever you retire this worktree. And this session's local branch is now behind `master`; nothing more to do here since the work is merged.
